They told me my weight was too low. \n\nI don't really see it, to be honest. To me, I just look lean... you know, like someone who's ready to work and stay quick on his feet. But they said I had to come see someone, so here I am.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror or think about your body, do you ever feel like you're overweight or worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really think I'm overweight, but... yeah, I definitely worry about gaining it. Like, if I eat rice or bread, or anything greasy, honestly, I just can't stop thinking about it. It feels like it's going to just sit on me right away. \n\nI usually end up checking the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. I just need to keep things tight, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food and feel like you can't stop, and if so, do you do anything afterward—like make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or exercise intensely—to make up for it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't... I don't lose control and eat a bunch of food all at once. It's actually the opposite for me. I just keep a tight lid on what I eat. So I don't ever need to make myself throw up or take pills or anything like that. I just stick to my routine and keep my portions small.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your current height and weight, so I can get a clearer picture of where you stand physically?\n\nPatient: I don't have the exact numbers on me, to be honest. The clinic nurses took all that down for the physical, but I didn't really memorize it. \n\nI know how tall I've always been, but as far as the weight goes... I just step on the scale at home to make sure it's not going up. I don't really pay attention to the exact number, just that it's staying down where I want it. The clinic just told me it was too low to clear me for the freight job, and left it at that.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to check your phone, a recent form, or call the clinic to get your exact height and weight measurements from that physical, so we can calculate your BMI together?\n\nPatient: I don't actually have any of the paperwork on me. And I never set up that... you know, that online patient portal thing on my phone that they always push, so I can't really look it up right now. \n\nI guess your office could call the clinic if you really need the exact numbers for your file. I don't mind if you do. I just know what they told me—that it was too low to clear me to go back on the freight line. But yeah, I don't have the exact measurements to give you right now.\n\nDoctor: Compared to your usual or highest adult weight, about how much weight have you lost, and over what period of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what my highest weight ever was. I haven't really kept track of it over the years. I know I've lost a good amount of weight over the past, I don't know, maybe six or eight months? I just know my clothes are a lot looser now, and people have mentioned it. But I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds I've lost. I just know it's been a steady drop since I started being more careful about what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Even without exact numbers, can you give me your best estimate of your current weight right now, in pounds?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't give you a good guess. Like I was saying, when I step on the scale at home, I'm just checking to see if the needle is lower than it was before. I... I don't actually look at the numbers on the dial. So I really don't know what it is in pounds right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

It feels like it's going to just sit on me right away. \n\nI usually end up checking the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. I just need to keep things tight, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food and feel like you can't stop, and if so, do you do anything afterward—like make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or exercise intensely—to make up for it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't... I don't lose control and eat a bunch of food all at once. It's actually the opposite for me. I just keep a tight lid on what I eat. So I don't ever need to make myself throw up or take pills or anything like that. I just stick to my routine and keep my portions small.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your current height and weight, so I can get a clearer picture of where you stand physically?\n\nPatient: I don't have the exact numbers on me, to be honest. The clinic nurses took all that down for the physical, but I didn't really memorize it. \n\nI know how tall I've always been, but as far as the weight goes... I just step on the scale at home to make sure it's not going up. I don't really pay attention to the exact number, just that it's staying down where I want it. The clinic just told me it was too low to clear me for the freight job, and left it at that.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to check your phone, a recent form, or call the clinic to get your exact height and weight measurements from that physical, so we can calculate your BMI together?\n\nPatient: I don't actually have any of the paperwork on me. And I never set up that... you know, that online patient portal thing on my phone that they always push, so I can't really look it up right now. \n\nI guess your office could call the clinic if you really need the exact numbers for your file. I don't mind if you do. I just know what they told me—that it was too low to clear me to go back on the freight line. But yeah, I don't have the exact measurements to give you right now.\n\nDoctor: Compared to your usual or highest adult weight, about how much weight have you lost, and over what period of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what my highest weight ever was. I haven't really kept track of it over the years. I know I've lost a good amount of weight over the past, I don't know, maybe six or eight months? I just know my clothes are a lot looser now, and people have mentioned it. But I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds I've lost. I just know it's been a steady drop since I started being more careful about what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Even without exact numbers, can you give me your best estimate of your current weight right now, in pounds?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't give you a good guess. Like I was saying, when I step on the scale at home, I'm just checking to see if the needle is lower than it was before. I... I don't actually look at the numbers on the dial. So I really don't know what it is in pounds right now.\n\nDoctor: Since you don't have the numbers, would it be alright if I have my office call the clinic directly to get your exact height and weight from that physical exam?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's fine. Like I said before, I don't mind if you call them to get the numbers for your file. You can go ahead and do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like feeling unusually cold all the time, dizziness or lightheadedness, thinning hair, or a lack of energy since you started losing weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any of that. I mean, I'm out moving freight all day, so I'm usually pretty warm. I haven't felt dizzy or had any issues with my hair thinning out. As for energy, I feel fine. I get through my shifts just like I always have. So, no, none of those things have been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you happen to have a weight listed on your driver's license or any other ID that might give us a more recent, official number?\n\nPatient: Well, my driver's license has a weight on it, but... it's definitely not recent. They just carry over the same number every time I renew it, so it's from years ago. I haven't updated it in a long time, so it wouldn't really tell you what I weigh right now.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I just need to keep things tight, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food and feel like you can't stop, and if so, do you do anything afterward—like make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or exercise intensely—to make up for it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't... I don't lose control and eat a bunch of food all at once. It's actually the opposite for me. I just keep a tight lid on what I eat. So I don't ever need to make myself throw up or take pills or anything like that. I just stick to my routine and keep my portions small.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your current height and weight, so I can get a clearer picture of where you stand physically?\n\nPatient: I don't have the exact numbers on me, to be honest. The clinic nurses took all that down for the physical, but I didn't really memorize it. \n\nI know how tall I've always been, but as far as the weight goes... I just step on the scale at home to make sure it's not going up. I don't really pay attention to the exact number, just that it's staying down where I want it. The clinic just told me it was too low to clear me for the freight job, and left it at that.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to check your phone, a recent form, or call the clinic to get your exact height and weight measurements from that physical, so we can calculate your BMI together?\n\nPatient: I don't actually have any of the paperwork on me. And I never set up that... you know, that online patient portal thing on my phone that they always push, so I can't really look it up right now. \n\nI guess your office could call the clinic if you really need the exact numbers for your file. I don't mind if you do. I just know what they told me—that it was too low to clear me to go back on the freight line. But yeah, I don't have the exact measurements to give you right now.\n\nDoctor: Compared to your usual or highest adult weight, about how much weight have you lost, and over what period of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what my highest weight ever was. I haven't really kept track of it over the years. I know I've lost a good amount of weight over the past, I don't know, maybe six or eight months? I just know my clothes are a lot looser now, and people have mentioned it. But I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds I've lost. I just know it's been a steady drop since I started being more careful about what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Even without exact numbers, can you give me your best estimate of your current weight right now, in pounds?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't give you a good guess. Like I was saying, when I step on the scale at home, I'm just checking to see if the needle is lower than it was before. I... I don't actually look at the numbers on the dial. So I really don't know what it is in pounds right now.\n\nDoctor: Since you don't have the numbers, would it be alright if I have my office call the clinic directly to get your exact height and weight from that physical exam?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's fine. Like I said before, I don't mind if you call them to get the numbers for your file. You can go ahead and do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like feeling unusually cold all the time, dizziness or lightheadedness, thinning hair, or a lack of energy since you started losing weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any of that. I mean, I'm out moving freight all day, so I'm usually pretty warm. I haven't felt dizzy or had any issues with my hair thinning out. As for energy, I feel fine. I get through my shifts just like I always have. So, no, none of those things have been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you happen to have a weight listed on your driver's license or any other ID that might give us a more recent, official number?\n\nPatient: Well, my driver's license has a weight on it, but... it's definitely not recent. They just carry over the same number every time I renew it, so it's from years ago. I haven't updated it in a long time, so it wouldn't really tell you what I weigh right now.\n\nDoctor: Did the clinic mention anything about your heart rate or blood pressure during that physical exam—like whether it was unusually low?\n\nPatient: No, they didn't say anything about that. I mean, they did the usual checks—put the cuff on my arm, listened to my chest—but the doctor there didn't mention my blood pressure or heart rate being low or anything. If there was an issue with it, they didn't tell me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.     "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n13\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.0, F50.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome in—I'm Dr. [Name]. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm here because the medical clinic for this logistics job I'm trying to get sent me. They wouldn't clear me for work. They told me my weight was too low. \n\nI don't really see it, to be honest. To me, I just look lean... you know, like someone who's ready to work and stay quick on his feet. But they said I had to come see someone, so here I am.\n\nDoctor: When you look in the mirror or think about your body, do you ever feel like you're overweight or worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really think I'm overweight, but... yeah, I definitely worry about gaining it. Like, if I eat rice or bread, or anything greasy, honestly, I just can't stop thinking about it. It feels like it's going to just sit on me right away. \n\nI usually end up checking the scale the next morning, or I'll see how my belt fits, just to make sure I didn't let myself go. I just need to keep things tight, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever have episodes where you eat a large amount of food and feel like you can't stop, and if so, do you do anything afterward—like make yourself vomit, use laxatives, or exercise intensely—to make up for it?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really do anything like that. I don't... I don't lose control and eat a bunch of food all at once. It's actually the opposite for me. I just keep a tight lid on what I eat. So I don't ever need to make myself throw up or take pills or anything like that. I just stick to my routine and keep my portions small.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me your current height and weight, so I can get a clearer picture of where you stand physically?\n\nPatient: I don't have the exact numbers on me, to be honest. The clinic nurses took all that down for the physical, but I didn't really memorize it. \n\nI know how tall I've always been, but as far as the weight goes... I just step on the scale at home to make sure it's not going up. I don't really pay attention to the exact number, just that it's staying down where I want it. The clinic just told me it was too low to clear me for the freight job, and left it at that.\n\nDoctor: Would you be willing to check your phone, a recent form, or call the clinic to get your exact height and weight measurements from that physical, so we can calculate your BMI together?\n\nPatient: I don't actually have any of the paperwork on me. And I never set up that... you know, that online patient portal thing on my phone that they always push, so I can't really look it up right now. \n\nI guess your office could call the clinic if you really need the exact numbers for your file. I don't mind if you do. I just know what they told me—that it was too low to clear me to go back on the freight line. But yeah, I don't have the exact measurements to give you right now.\n\nDoctor: Compared to your usual or highest adult weight, about how much weight have you lost, and over what period of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what my highest weight ever was. I haven't really kept track of it over the years. I know I've lost a good amount of weight over the past, I don't know, maybe six or eight months? I just know my clothes are a lot looser now, and people have mentioned it. But I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds I've lost. I just know it's been a steady drop since I started being more careful about what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Even without exact numbers, can you give me your best estimate of your current weight right now, in pounds?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't give you a good guess. Like I was saying, when I step on the scale at home, I'm just checking to see if the needle is lower than it was before. I... I don't actually look at the numbers on the dial. So I really don't know what it is in pounds right now.\n\nDoctor: Since you don't have the numbers, would it be alright if I have my office call the clinic directly to get your exact height and weight from that physical exam?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's fine. Like I said before, I don't mind if you call them to get the numbers for your file. You can go ahead and do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like feeling unusually cold all the time, dizziness or lightheadedness, thinning hair, or a lack of energy since you started losing weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any of that. I mean, I'm out moving freight all day, so I'm usually pretty warm. I haven't felt dizzy or had any issues with my hair thinning out. As for energy, I feel fine. I get through my shifts just like I always have. So, no, none of those things have been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you happen to have a weight listed on your driver's license or any other ID that might give us a more recent, official number?\n\nPatient: Well, my driver's license has a weight on it, but... it's definitely not recent. They just carry over the same number every time I renew it, so it's from years ago. I haven't updated it in a long time, so it wouldn't really tell you what I weigh right now.\n\nDoctor: Did the clinic mention anything about your heart rate or blood pressure during that physical exam—like whether it was unusually low?\n\nPatient: No, they didn't say anything about that. I mean, they did the usual checks—put the cuff on my arm, listened to my chest—but the doctor there didn't mention my blood pressure or heart rate being low or anything. If there was an issue with it, they didn't tell me.\n\nDoctor: Since we don't have a recent number, would you be willing to step on the scale here in my office right now so we can get an accurate current weight and height?\n\nPatient: No, I... I really don't want to do that. I know you're just trying to get a number, but I'm not comfortable stepping on a scale right now. I'd rather not.\n\nDoctor: What pant or waist size do you wear now, and how does that compare to the size you used to wear before you started losing weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about the exact numbers. I haven't bought new clothes in a long time. I just wear my usual work pants, and... well, I just have to pull my belt a lot tighter than I used to. They're definitely a lot looser on me now, but I couldn't tell you what my actual waist size is right now compared to back then.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

But yeah, I don't have the exact measurements to give you right now.\n\nDoctor: Compared to your usual or highest adult weight, about how much weight have you lost, and over what period of time?\n\nPatient: I'm not exactly sure what my highest weight ever was. I haven't really kept track of it over the years. I know I've lost a good amount of weight over the past, I don't know, maybe six or eight months? I just know my clothes are a lot looser now, and people have mentioned it. But I couldn't give you an exact number of pounds I've lost. I just know it's been a steady drop since I started being more careful about what I eat.\n\nDoctor: Even without exact numbers, can you give me your best estimate of your current weight right now, in pounds?\n\nPatient: I honestly couldn't give you a good guess. Like I was saying, when I step on the scale at home, I'm just checking to see if the needle is lower than it was before. I... I don't actually look at the numbers on the dial. So I really don't know what it is in pounds right now.\n\nDoctor: Since you don't have the numbers, would it be alright if I have my office call the clinic directly to get your exact height and weight from that physical exam?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's fine. Like I said before, I don't mind if you call them to get the numbers for your file. You can go ahead and do that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any physical symptoms like feeling unusually cold all the time, dizziness or lightheadedness, thinning hair, or a lack of energy since you started losing weight?\n\nPatient: I haven't really noticed any of that. I mean, I'm out moving freight all day, so I'm usually pretty warm. I haven't felt dizzy or had any issues with my hair thinning out. As for energy, I feel fine. I get through my shifts just like I always have. So, no, none of those things have been an issue.\n\nDoctor: Do you happen to have a weight listed on your driver's license or any other ID that might give us a more recent, official number?\n\nPatient: Well, my driver's license has a weight on it, but... it's definitely not recent. I'd rather not.\n\nDoctor: What pant or waist size do you wear now, and how does that compare to the size you used to wear before you started losing weight?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about the exact numbers.
